# LE Lab — threshold adjudication sheet

Generated by `tools/lab-threshold-sweep.mjs`. One row per corpus pair that crossed an
admission threshold between the captured baseline and the current tree. The rightmost
column is the ruling, rendered from `tests/fixtures/threshold-neighbors.json` so this
sheet and the fixture the suite enforces cannot disagree.

**The canon moved in this run.** Crossings below are attributable to the doctrine
change as well as to any scoring change: adding entries moves IDF for every pair,
and new entries enter the candidate set from a prior score of zero.

**Adjudication open — 4052 of 4471 crossings outstanding.**

```
analyzer   2.6.17 -> 2.6.17
config     bt0a7p -> bt0a7p
canon      1.0.0+93e06ff160d9 -> 1.0.0+3b3901828bc0  (doctrine moved)
population 2426 retained passages x 556 entries = 1348856 pairs
changed    96368   49969 down / 46399 up
candidateScoreFloor  0.08  3871 gain / 181 loss
minWeakScore         0.25  256 gain / 153 loss
minCredibleScore     0.43  5 gain / 5 loss
```

**How to rule.** ACCEPT means the new side is the right side — the pair crossed because
the old score was resting on a fragment that was never a concept. REJECT means the pair
belonged where it was, and the fix has cost something real. A REJECT does **not** become a
threshold change: thresholds are not retuned to un-cross a pair, because that trades one
adjudicated case for every un-adjudicated one. It becomes a targeted fixture pinning the
pre-fix outcome and an entry in the release report saying what the fix cost.

Record each verdict in `tests/fixtures/threshold-neighbors.json` under `rulings`, keyed
`<unitId>|<canonId>|<threshold>`, and set `adjudicationOpen: false` when none are left
PENDING. The suite enforces that pairing.
